Note: Item O of Sec. 133 must be 
correlated with Sec. 193. Thus, tax 
exemptions of GOCCs created before 
the effectivity of the 1991 LGC are 
revoked. Conversely, tax exemptions of 
GOCCs created after the effectivity of 
the 1991 LGC are not revoked. 
15. 
Taxes 
on 
the 
gross 
receipts 
of 
transportation 
contractors 
and 
persons 
engaged in the transportation of passengers or 
freight by hire and common carriers by air, land, 
or water, except as provided in this Code. 
Note: An examination of the above 
enumeration reveals that those taxes, 
charges, and fees already imposed and 
collected by the National Government 
such as income taxes, estate taxes, 
donor’s taxes, documentary stamp 
taxes. Under the Reservation Rule or 
Exclusionary Rule, the LGUs cannot 
exercise taxing powers reserved to the 
National Government. 
3. Validity of Local Tax Ordinance – 
Sec. 187 
Any question on the constitutionality or 
legality 
of 
tax 
ordinances 
or revenue 
measures may be raised on appeal within thirty 
(30) days from the effectivity thereof to the 
Secretary of Justice who shall render a 
decision within sixty (60) days from the date of 
receipt of the appeal:  
 
Provided, however, that such appeal shall not 
have the effect of suspending the effectivity of 
the ordinance and the accrual and payment of 
the tax, fee, or charge levied therein: Provided, 
finally, That within thirty (30) days after receipt 
of the decision or the lapse of the sixty-day 
period without the Secretary of Justice acting 
upon the appeal, the aggrieved party may file 
appropriate proceedings with a court of 
competent jurisdiction. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


<!-- PAGE 321 -->

 
 
 
 
 
314 
4.  Assessment 
and 
Collection 
of 
Local Taxes – Sec. 194 
a. Remedies of Local Government Units 
(LGUs) 
Local government units may enforce the 
collection of local taxes, fees, or charges 
through administrative or judicial action. 
Collection may be pursued only after a valid 
assessment and must be instituted within the 
period prescribed by law. If the assessment 
is made within the allowable prescriptive 
period, the LGU may thereafter avail of 
summary administrative remedies or file an 
action in court to collect the assessed tax, 
subject 
to 
the 
prescriptive 
periods 
for 
collection. 
b. Prescriptive Period 
1. Local taxes, fees, or charges - shall be 
assessed within five (5) years from the date 
they became due.  
 
2. Taxes, fees, or charges which accrued 
before 
the 
effectivity 
of 
the 
Local 
Government Code - may be assessed within 
three (3) years from the date they became 
due. 
 
3. Fraud or intent to evade the payment of 
local taxes, fees, or charges - assessment 
may be made within ten (10) years from 
discovery of the fraud or intent to evade 
payment. 
 
Once assessed, local taxes, fees, or charges 
may be collected within five (5) years from the 
date of assessment, whether by administrative 
or judicial action. Taxes, fees, or charges 
assessed before the effectivity of the Code 
may be collected within three (3) years from 
the date of assessment. 
 
The running of the prescriptive periods for 
assessment or collection shall be suspended 
during the time when: 
 
(1) the treasurer is legally prevented 
from making the 
assessment 
or 
collection; 
(2) 
the 
taxpayer 
requests 
a 
reinvestigation and executes a written 
waiver before the expiration of the 
period to assess or collect; or 
(3) the taxpayer is out of the country or 
otherwise cannot be located. 
 
c. Injunction 
Unlike the National Internal Revenue Code, the 
Local Tax Code does not contain any specific 
provision prohibiting courts from enjoining the 
collection of local taxes. Such statutory lapse or 
intent, however it may be viewed, may have 
allowed preliminary injunction where local 
taxes are involved but cannot negate the 
procedural rules and requirements under Rule 
58 (Rules of Court). [Angeles City v. Angeles 
City Electric Corporation (2010)] 
5. Taxpayers’ Remedies 
a. Protest – Sec. 195 
When the local treasurer or his duly authorized 
representative finds that correct taxes, fees, or 
charges have not been paid, he shall issue a 
notice of assessment stating the nature of the 
tax, fee, or charge, the amount of deficiency, 
the surcharges, interests and penalties.  
 
Within sixty (60) days from the receipt of the 
notice of assessment, the taxpayer may file a 
written protest with the local treasurer 
contesting the assessment; otherwise, the 
assessment shall become final and executory.  
 
The local treasurer shall decide the protest 
within sixty (60) days from the time of its 
filing. If the local treasurer finds the protest to 
be wholly or partly meritorious, he shall issue a 
notice cancelling wholly or partially the 
assessment.  


<!-- PAGE 322 -->

 
 
 
 
 
315 
 
However, if the local treasurer finds the 
assessment to be wholly or partly correct, he 
shall deny the protest wholly or partly with 
notice to the taxpayer.  
 
The taxpayer shall have thirty (30) days from 
the receipt of the denial of the protest or from 
the lapse of the sixty (60) day period 
prescribed herein within which to appeal with 
the court of competent jurisdiction otherwise 
the assessment becomes conclusive and 
unappealable. 
 
b. Refund or Credit – Sec. 196 
No case or proceeding shall be maintained in 
any court for the recovery of any tax, fee, or 
charge erroneously or illegally collected until a 
written claim for refund or credit has been 
filed with the local treasurer.  
 
No case or proceeding shall be entertained in 
any court after the expiration of two (2) years 
from the date of the payment of such tax, fee, 
or charge, or from the date the taxpayer is 
entitled to a refund or credit. 
 
c. Court Action 
After exhaustion of administrative remedies, 
the taxpayer may file the appropriate judicial 
action before the court of competent 
jurisdiction, generally the Regional Trial 
Court, to contest: 
 
 The denial of a protest against a local 
tax assessment; or 
 
 The denial or inaction on a claim for 
refund or tax credit. 
 
Such court action must be filed within the 
periods prescribed under Sections 195 and 
196 of the Local Government Code of 1991; 
otherwise, the assessment or collection 
becomes final and unassailable. 
 
6.  Local Taxes  
a. Professional Tax – Sec. 139 
 
(a) The province may levy an annual 
professional tax on each person engaged in 
the exercise or practice of his profession 
requiring government examination at such 
amount and reasonable classification as the 
sangguniang panlalawigan may determine but 
shall in no case exceed Three hundred pesos 
(P300.00). 
 
(b) Every person legally authorized to practice 
his profession shall pay the professional tax to 
the province where he practices his profession 
or where he maintains his principal office in 
case he practices his profession in several 
places: Provided, however, That such person 
who has paid the corresponding professional 
tax shall be entitled to practice his profession 
in any part of the Philippines without being 
subjected to any other national or local tax, 
license, or fee for the practice of such 
profession. 
 
(c) Any individual or corporation employing a 
person subject to professional tax shall require 
payment by that person of the tax on his 
profession before employment and annually 
thereafter. 
 
(d) The professional tax shall be payable 
annually, on or before the thirty-first (31st) day 
of January. Any person first beginning to 
practice a profession after the month of 
January must, however, pay the full tax before 
engaging therein. A line of profession does not 
become exempt even if conducted with some 
other profession for which the tax has been 
paid. Professionals exclusively employed in 
the government shall be exempt from the 
payment of this tax. 
 
(e) Any person subject to the professional tax 
shall write in deeds, receipts, prescriptions, 
reports, books of account, plans and designs, 


<!-- PAGE 323 -->

 
 
 
 
 
316 
surveys and maps, as the case may be, the 
number of the official receipt issued to him. 
 
 
b. Local Business Tax – Secs. 143, 145, and 
146 
 
The municipality may impose taxes on the 
following businesses: 
 
(a) On manufacturers, assemblers, repackers, 
processors, brewers, distillers, rectifiers, and 
compounders of liquors, distilled spirits, and 
wines or manufacturers of any article of 
commerce of whatever kind or nature.  
 
(b) On wholesalers, distributors, or dealers in 
any article of commerce of whatever kind or 
nature. 
 
(c) On exporters, and on manufacturers , 
millers, producers, wholesalers, distributors, 
dealers or retailers of essential commodities 
enumerated hereunder at a rate not exceeding 
one-half (½) of the rates prescribed under 
subsection (a), (b) and (d) of Section 143 of RA 
7160, as amended. 
 
(1) Rice and corn; 
(2) Wheat or cassava flour, meat, dairy 
products, 
locally 
manufactured, 
processed or preserved food, sugar, 
salt and other agricultural, marine, and 
fresh water products, whether in their 
original state or not; 
(3) Cooking oil and cooking gas; 
(4) Laundry soap, detergents, and 
medicine; 
(5) Agricultural implements. equipment 
and post-harvest facilities, fertilizers, 
pesticides, insecticides, herbicides and 
other farm inputs; 
(6) Poultry feeds and other animal 
feeds; 
(7) School supplies; and 
(8) Cement. 
(d) On retailers 
 
 
Retirement of Business 
 
A business subject to tax pursuant to the 
preceding sections shall, upon termination 
thereof, submit a sworn statement of its gross 
sales or receipts for the current year. If the tax 
paid during the year be less than the tax due 
on said gross sales or receipts of the current 
year, the difference shall be paid before the 
business is considered officially retired. 
 
c. Community Tax – Secs. 157–160 
 
Individuals Liable to Community Tax 
 
Every inhabitant of the Philippines eighteen 
(18) years of age or over shall pay the 
community tax if he or she falls under any of 
the following: 
1. Has been regularly employed on a 
wage or salary basis for at least thirty 
(30) consecutive working days during 
any calendar year; 
2. Is engaged in business or occupation; 
3. Owns real property with an aggregate 
assessed value of One Thousand 
Pesos (₱1,000.00) or more; or 
4. Is required by law to file an income tax 
return. 
 
Amount of Community Tax 
 
a. An annual basic tax of Five Pesos (₱5.00); 
and 
 
b. An additional tax of One Peso (₱1.00) for 
every One Thousand Pesos (₱1,000.00) of 
income, whether from business, exercise of 
profession, or from property, which shall in no 
case 
exceed 
Five 
Thousand 
Pesos 
(₱5,000.00). 
 
In the case of husband and wife, the additional 
tax herein imposed shall be based upon the 
total property owned by them and the total 
gross receipts or earnings derived by them. 
 
 
 


<!-- PAGE 324 -->

 
 
 
 
 
317 
Juridical Persons Liable to Community Tax 
 
Every corporation no matter how created or 
organized, whether domestic or resident 
foreign, engaged in or doing business in the 
Philippines shall pay an annual community tax 
of Five hundred pesos (P500.00) and an 
annual additional tax, which, in no case, shall 
exceed Ten thousand pesos (P10,000.00) in 
accordance with the following schedule: 
 
(1) For every Five thousand pesos (P5,000.00) 
worth of real property in the Philippines owned 
by it during the preceding year based on the 
valuation used for the payment of real property 
tax under existing laws, found in the 
assessment rolls of the city or municipality 
where the real property is situated - Two pesos 
(P2.00); and 
 
(2) For every Five thousand pesos (P5,000.00) 
of gross receipts or earnings derived by it from 
its business in the Philippines during the 
preceding year - Two pesos (P2.00). 
 
The dividends received by a corporation from 
another corporation however shall, for the 
purpose of the additional tax, be considered as 
part of the gross receipts or earnings of said 
corporation. 
 
Exemptions. The following are exempt from 
the community tax: 
 
(1) Diplomatic and consular representatives; 
and 
(2) Transient visitors when their stay in the 
Philippines does not exceed three (3) months. 
 
Place of Payment 
 
The community tax shall be paid in the place of 
residence of the individual, or in the place 
where the principal office of the juridical entity 
is located. 
 
 
 
 
d. Real Property Taxation (Book II, Title 
II) 
 
i. Fundamental principles; nature; power 
to levy 
 
Fundamental Principles. The appraisal, 
assessment, levy and collection of real 
property tax shall be guided by the following 
fundamental principles: 
 
(a) Real property shall be appraised at its 
current and fair market value; 
(b) Real property shall be classified for 
assessment purposes on the basis of its actual 
use; 
(c) Real property shall be assessed on the 
basis of a uniform classification within each 
local government unit; 
(d) The appraisal, assessment, levy and 
collection of real property tax shall not be let to 
any private person; and 
(e) The appraisal and assessment of real 
property shall be equitable. 
 
 
Nature of Real Property Taxation (Local) 
 
It is a local ad valorem tax imposed on real 
property, including land, buildings, machinery, 
and other improvements, based on their 
assessed value.  
 
It is a direct tax on ownership or beneficial 
use, levied annually, and attaches to the 
property itself rather than to the person of the 
owner. The tax is exercised by local 
government units pursuant to their delegated 
taxing power and serves as a primary and 
stable source of local government revenue. 
 
It is implemented through a structured process 
of valuation → assessment → notice → 
collection, 
with 
specialized 
rules 
and 
remedies 
distinct 
from 
other 
local 
business/privilege taxes. 
 
 


<!-- PAGE 325 -->

 
 
 
 
 
318 
Power to Levy Real Property Tax 
 
A province or city or a municipality within the 
Metropolitan Manila Area my levy an annual ad 
valorem tax on real property such as land, 
building, machinery, and other improvement 
not hereinafter specifically exempted. 
 
Rates of Levy. A province or city or a 
municipality within the Metropolitan Manila 
Area shall fix a uniform rate of basic real 
property tax applicable to their respective 
localities as follows: 
 
(a) In the case of a province, at the rate not 
exceeding one percent (1%) of the assessed 
value of real property; and 
(b) In the case of a city or a municipality within 
the Metropolitan Manila Area, at the rate not 
exceeding two percent (2%) of the assessed 
value of real property. 
 
 
ii. Exemption from real property tax – 
Sec. 234 
 
The following are exempted from payment of 
the real property tax: 
 
(a) Real property owned by the 
Republic of the Philippines or any of its 
political subdivisions except when the 
beneficial 
use 
thereof 
has 
been 
granted, for consideration or otherwise, 
to a taxable person; 
 
(b) Charitable institutions, churches, 
parsonages or convents appurtenant 
thereto, 
mosques, 
non-profit 
or 
religious cemeteries and all lands, 
buildings, and improvements actually, 
directly, and exclusively used for 
religious, charitable or educational 
purposes; 
 
(c) All machineries and equipment that 
are actually, directly and exclusively 
used by local water districts and 
government 
owned 
or 
controlled 
corporations engaged in the supply and 
distribution of water and/or generation 
and transmission of electric power; 
 
(d) All real property owned by duly 
registered cooperatives as provided for 
under R.A. No. 6938; and 
 
(e) Machinery and equipment used for 
pollution control and environmental 
protection. 
 
Except as provided, any exemption from 
payment of real property tax previously 
granted to, or presently enjoyed by, all 
persons, whether natural or juridical, including 
all 
government-owned 
or 
controlled 
corporations are hereby withdrawn upon the 
effectivity of the Local Gov’t Code. 
 
iii. Appraisal and assessment 
 
"Appraisal" is the act or process of 
determining the value of property as of a 
specified date for a specific purpose. 
 
"Assessment" is the act or process of 
determining the value of a property, or 
proportion thereof subject to tax, including the 
discovery, listing, classification, and appraisal 
of properties. 
 
All real property, whether taxable or exempt, 
shall be appraised at the current and fair 
market value prevailing in the locality where 
the property is situated. The Department of 
Finance shall promulgate the necessary rules 
and regulations for the classification, appraisal, 
and assessment of real property pursuant to 
the provisions of the Local Gov’t Code, as 
amended. 
 
iv. Collection 
 
The collection of the real property tax with 
interest thereon and related expenses, and the 
enforcement of the remedies provided, shall be 
the responsibility of the city or municipal 
treasurer concerned. 


<!-- PAGE 326 -->

 
 
 
 
 
319 
The city or municipal treasurer may deputize 
the barangay treasurer to collect all taxes on 
real property located in the barangay: 
Provided, That the barangay treasurer is 
properly bonded for the purpose: Provided, 
further, That the premium on the bond shall be 
paid by the city or municipal government 
concerned. 
 
 
Collection of Real Property Tax Through 
the Courts 
 
The local government unit concerned may 
enforce the collection of the basic real property 
tax or any other tax levied under this Title by 
civil action in any court of competent 
jurisdiction. The civil action shall be filed by the 
local treasurer within the period prescribed in 
Section 270 of the Local Gov’t Code. 
 
 
v. Taxpayers’ remedies 
(a) Against assessment 
   (1) Payment under protest; 
exceptions 
 
 
GR: No protest shall be entertained unless the 
taxpayer first pays the tax. There shall be 
annotated on the tax receipts the words "paid 
under protest" [Sec. 252, LGC]. When the 
taxpayer questions the excessiveness or 
reasonableness of the assessment, the 
taxpayer is required to first pay the tax due 
before his protest can be entertained [NPC v. 
Provincial Treasurer of Benguet, G.R. No. 
209303 (2016)].  
 
XPN: “Payment under protest” is not a 
prerequisite when the issue is the legality or 
validity of the assessment. Certainly, it would 
be unjust to require the realty owner to first pay 
the tax, the validity of which he precisely 
questions, before he can lodge a complaint to 
the court [NPC v. Municipal Government of 
Navotas, G.R. 192300 (2014)]. 
 
 
(2) Compromise of assessment 
 
The idea of “compromise of assessment” in 
real property taxation is derived from the 
administrative powers of the local treasurer 
and assessor, particularly: 
  
Section 195, LGC - It allows the local treasurer 
to “cancel wholly or partially an assessment if 
the 
protest 
is 
found 
wholly 
or 
partly 
meritorious.” 
 
A partial cancellation or adjustment of the 
assessment functions in substance like a 
compromise, although the law frames it as 
resolution of a protest. 
 
b) Against valuation of property 
   (1) Appeal to the Local Board of  
Assessment Appeals 
 
 Any owner or person having legal interest in 
the property who is not satisfied with the action 
of the provincial, city or municipal assessor in 
the assessment of his property may, within 
sixty (60) days from the date of receipt of the 
written notice of assessment, appeal to the 
Board of Assessment Appeals of the provincial 
or city by filing a petition under oath in the form 
prescribed for the purpose, together with 
copies of the tax declarations and such 
affidavits or documents submitted in support of 
the appeal. 
 
Action by the Local Board of Assessment 
Appeals 
 
(a) The Board shall decide the appeal within 
one hundred twenty (120) days from the date 
of receipt of such appeal. The Board, after 
hearing, shall render its decision based on 
substantial evidence or such relevant evidence 
on record as a reasonable mind might accept 
as adequate to support the conclusion. 
 
(b) In the exercise of its appellate jurisdiction, 
the Board shall have the power to summon 


<!-- PAGE 327 -->

 
 
 
 
 
320 
witnesses, administer oaths, conduct ocular 
inspection, 
take 
depositions, 
and 
issue 
subpoena and subpoena duces tecum. The 
proceedings of the Board shall be conducted 
solely for the purpose of ascertaining the facts 
without necessarily adhering to technical rules 
applicable in judicial proceedings. 
 
(c) The secretary of the Board shall furnish the 
owner of the property or the person having 
legal interest therein and the provincial or city 
assessor with a copy of the decision of the 
Board. In case the provincial or city assessor 
concurs in the revision or the assessment, it 
shall be his duty to notify the owner of the 
property or the person having legal interest 
therein of such fact using the form prescribed 
for the purpose. The owner of the property or 
the person having legal interest therein or the 
assessor who is not satisfied with the decision 
of the Board, may, within thirty (30) days after 
receipt of the decision of said Board, appeal to 
the Central Board of Assessment Appeals, as 
herein provided. The decision of the Central 
Board shall be final and executory. 
 
   (2) Appeal to the Central Board of 
Assessment Appeals 
 
An appeal may be taken to the Central Board 
of Assessment Appeals from decisions of the 
Local Board of Assessment Appeals.  
 
The Central Board has appellate jurisdiction 
over all assessment cases decided by the 
Local Board of Assessment Appeals. It is 
composed of a Chairman and two (2) Members 
appointed by the President, with fixed terms 
and qualifications prescribed by law.  
 
The Board may hear and decide appeals 
directly or through duly appointed Hearing 
Officers for Luzon, Visayas, and Mindanao, 
who possess the qualifications of Municipal 
Trial Court Judges.  
 
In the exercise of its powers and duties, the 
Central Board may organize its staff and adopt 
its own rules and regulations. 
 
(3) Effect of payment of taxes 
 
Note: The Local Government Code, after its 
discussion on appeals to the Local Board of 
Assessment Appeals and the Central Board of 
Assessment Appeals, does not expressly mention 
the “Effect of payment of taxes.”  
 
Instead, it discusses, under Section 231, the “Effect 
of Appeal on the Payment of Real Property Tax.” In 
the 2026 syllabus, what is required is the “Effect of 
payment of taxes.”  
 
However, 
by 
analogy 
and 
considering 
the 
chronological order of the topics, the proper 
reference should be the “Effect of Appeal on the 
Payment of Real Property Tax.” Accordingly, it is the 
latter that is discussed below. 
 
Effect of “Appeal” on the Payment of Real 
Property Tax 
 
Appeal on assessments of real property made 
under the provisions of the LGC shall, in no 
case, 
suspend 
the 
collection 
of 
the 
corresponding realty taxes on the property 
involved as assessed by the provincial or city 
assessor, without prejudice to subsequent 
adjustment depending upon the final outcome 
of the appeal. 
 
D. Judicial Remedies 
 
1. Court of Tax Appeals (CTA) 
[R.A. 1125, as amended by R.A. No. 3457 and 
further amended by R.A. 9282 and R.A. 9503, and 
A.M. No. 05-11-07-CTA or the Revised Rules of the 
Court of Tax Appeals (RRCTA)] 
 
The exclusive original or appellate jurisdiction 
of the CTA over civil and criminal cases is 
determined by the nature of the case, the 
government agency involved, and the principal 
amount of taxes and fees claimed, as 


<!-- PAGE 328 -->

 
 
 
 
 
321 
expressly provided in RA No. 1125, as 
amended by RA Nos. 9282 and 9503, and the 
Revised Rules of the Court of Tax Appeals 
(RRCTA). 
 
a. Exclusive original and appellate 
jurisdiction over civil cases 
 
Exclusive Original Jurisdiction 
 
The Court of Tax Appeals, sitting in Divisions, 
shall exercise exclusive original jurisdiction 
over tax collection cases involving final and 
executory assessments for taxes, fees, 
charges, and penalties, where the principal 
amount of taxes and fees, exclusive of charges 
and penalties, claimed is one million pesos 
or more [Sec. 3(c)(1), Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
 
Exclusive Appellate Jurisdiction 
 
If the principal amount of taxes and fees, 
exclusive of charges and penalties, claimed is 
less than one million pesos or no specified 
amount is claimed, jurisdiction over tax 
collection cases shall be exercised by the 
proper MTC, MeTC, or RTC, depending on the 
respective jurisdiction, and the jurisdiction of 
the CTA in these cases shall be appellate [Sec. 
7(b)(2)(c), RA No. 9282]. 
 
The CTA in Divisions shall also exercise 
exclusive appellate jurisdiction to review by 
appeal judgments, resolutions, or orders of the 
RTCs in tax collection cases originally decided 
by them in the exercise of their original 
jurisdiction [Sec. 3(c)(2), Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
The CTA in Divisions shall further exercise 
exclusive original or appellate jurisdiction 
to review by appeal the following civil 
cases: 
 
1. Decisions of the Commissioner of Internal 
Revenue 
in 
cases 
involving 
disputed 
assessments, refunds of internal revenue 
taxes, fees, or other charges, penalties 
imposed in relation thereto, or other matters 
arising under the NIRC or other laws 
administered by the BIR, including cases of 
inaction by the CIR on disputed assessments 
within the 180-day period [Sec. 3(a)(1) and (2), 
Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
2. Decisions of the Commissioner of Customs 
in cases involving liability for customs duties, 
fees or other charges, seizure, detention or 
release of property affected, fines, forfeitures, 
or other penalties, and other matters arising 
under the Customs Law or other laws 
administered by the Bureau of Customs, 
including automatic review cases [Sec. 3(a)(3), 
Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
3. Decisions of the Secretary of Finance in 
customs cases elevated for automatic review 
[Sec. 3(a)(3), Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
4. Decisions of the Secretary of Trade and 
Industry and the Secretary of Agriculture, in 
cases 
involving 
dumping, 
countervailing 
duties, 
and 
safeguard 
measures 
under 
Republic Act No. 8800, where either party may 
appeal the decision to impose or not to impose 
such duties [Sec. 3(a)(4), Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
5. Decisions of the Central Board of 
Assessment Appeals (CBAA) in the exercise of 
its 
appellate 
jurisdiction 
involving 
the 
assessment and taxation of real property [Sec. 
3(a)(5), Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
The Court En Banc shall exercise exclusive 
appellate jurisdiction to review by appeal 
decisions or resolutions on motions for 
reconsideration or new trial of the CTA in 
Divisions [Sec. 2(c), Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
b. Exclusive original and appellate 
jurisdiction over criminal cases 
 
Exclusive Original Jurisdiction 
 
The Court of Tax Appeals, sitting in Divisions, 
shall exercise exclusive original jurisdiction 
over criminal offenses arising from violations of 


<!-- PAGE 329 -->

 
 
 
 
 
322 
the NIRC or the Tariff and Customs Code, 
and other laws administered by the BIR or 
the Bureau of Customs, where the principal 
amount of taxes and fees, exclusive of charges 
and penalties, claimed is one million pesos or 
more [Sec. 3(b)(1), Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
Exclusive Appellate Jurisdiction 
 
Where the principal amount of taxes and fees, 
exclusive of charges and penalties, claimed is 
less than one million pesos or where no 
specified amount is claimed, jurisdiction over 
such criminal cases shall be exercised by the 
regular courts, and the jurisdiction of the CTA 
shall be appellate [Sec. 3(b)(2), Rule 4, 
RRCTA]. 
 
The CTA in Divisions shall exercise exclusive 
appellate 
jurisdiction 
over 
appeals 
from 
judgments, resolutions, or orders of the RTCs 
in criminal cases involving violations of the 
NIRC or the Tariff and Customs Code, and 
other laws administered by the BIR or the 
Bureau of Customs, originally decided by the 
RTCs in the exercise of their original jurisdiction 
[Sec. 3(b)(2), Rule 4, RRCTA]. 
 
The Court En Banc shall exercise exclusive 
appellate jurisdiction to review by appeal 
decisions or resolutions on motions for 
reconsideration or new trial of the CTA in 
Divisions in criminal cases [Sec. 2(c), Rule 4, 
RRCTA]. 
 
2. Procedures 
 
a. Action for collection of taxes 
 
i. National taxes 
 
The remedies for the collection of internal 
revenue taxes, fees or charges, and any 
increment thereto resulting from delinquency 
can be through the institution of a civil or 
criminal action [Sec. 205, NIRC]. 
 
When this remedy is resorted to The tax 
assessment becomes final and executory 
because of the failure to appeal. Even pending 
decision of the administrative protest [CIR v. 
Union Shipping, G.R. No. L 66160 (1990)] 
 
 
ii. Local Taxes 
 
The LGU concerned may enforce the 
collection of delinquent taxes, fees, charges or 
other revenues by civil action in any court of 
competent jurisdiction (MTC/RTC depending 
on the jurisdictional amount). The civil action 
shall be filed by the local treasurer [Sec. 183, 
LGC]. 
 
Prescriptive Period 
 
Local taxes, fees, or charges shall be assessed 
within five (5) years from the date they became 
due. (Sec. 194[a], LGC) 
 
No action for the collection of such taxes, fees, 
or charges, whether administrative or judicial, 
shall be instituted after the expiration of such 
period. (Sec. 194[a], LGC) 
 
In case of fraud or intent to evade the payment 
of taxes, fees, or charges, the same may be 
assessed within ten (10) years from discovery 
of the fraud or intent to evade payment. (Sec. 
194[b], LGC) 
 
Local taxes, fees, or charges may be collected 
within five (5) years from the date of 
assessment by administrative or judicial action. 
(Sec. 194[c], LGC) 
 
No judicial or administrative action for collection 
can be instituted after lapse of the period for 
assessment, except when there is fraud or 
intent to evade tax. (Sec. 194[c], LGC) 
 


<!-- PAGE 330 -->

 
 
 
 
 
323 
The running of the periods of prescription shall 
be suspended for the time during which: 
 
1. The treasurer is legally prevented from 
making the assessment or collection; 
2. The 
taxpayer 
requests 
for 
a 
reinvestigation and executes a waiver in 
writing before the expiration of the 
period within which to assess or collect; 
and 
3. The taxpayer is out of the country or 
otherwise cannot be located [Sec. 
194(d), LGC]. 
 
b. Civil cases 
      i. Who may appeal, mode of appeal, 
and effect of appeal 
 
 
Appeal to CTA Division 
 
1. A party aggrieved or adversely 
affected by the decision or ruling of: 
a. CIR; 
b. Commissioner of Customs; 
c. Secretary of Finance; 
d. Secretary of Trade and Industry; 
e. Secretary of Agriculture; or 
f. RTC exercising original jurisdiction. 
 
2. The appeal may be taken within thirty 
(30) days from receipt of a copy of the 
decision or ruling, or from the 
expiration of the period fixed by law for 
the Commissioner to decide, to the 
Court of Tax Appeals Division. 
 
Mode of Appeal: Rule 42 
 
An aggrieved party may file a motion for 
reconsideration or a new trial within fifteen (15) 
days from receipt of a copy of the decision. 
 
 
 
Appeal to CTA en banc 
 
A party adversely affected by a decision or 
resolution of a Division of the Court on a motion 
for reconsideration or new trial may appeal 
within fifteen (15) days from receipt of a copy 
of the decision. 
 
Mode of Appeal: Rule 43 
 
A party adversely affected by a decision or 
ruling of the CTA en banc and the RTC in the 
exercise of their appellate jurisdiction may 
appeal within thirty (30) days from receipt of a 
copy of the decision. 
 
 
    ii. Suspension of collection of taxes 
 
GR: No appeal taken to the Court shall 
suspend the payment, levy, distraint, or sale of 
any property of the taxpayer for the satisfaction 
of his tax liability as provided under existing 
laws [Sec. 1, Rule 10, RRCTA]. 
 
Rationale: Lifeblood theory [Angeles 
City 
v. 
Angeles 
City 
Electric 
Corporation, G.R. No. 166134 (2010)] 
 
XPN: Where the collection of the amount of the 
taxpayer’s liability, sought by means of a 
demand for payment, by levy, distraint or sale 
of any property of the taxpayer, or by whatever 
means, as provided under existing laws, may 
jeopardize the interest of the Government or 
the taxpayer, an interested party may file a 
motion for the suspension of the collection of 
the tax liability [Sec. 11, R.A. No. 1125, as 
amended].  
 
Requisites:  
 
1. The taxpayer may be required to deposit the 
amount claimed or to file a surety bond for not 
more than double the amount with the Court 
[Sec. 11, R.A. 1125];  
 


<!-- PAGE 331 -->

 
 
 
 
 
324 
2. In the opinion of the CTA, the collection may 
jeopardize the interest of the Government 
and/or the taxpayer [Sec. 2, Rule 10, RRCTA];  
 
3. The motion for the suspension of the 
collection of tax shall be verified and shall state 
clearly and distinctly the facts and the grounds 
relied upon in support of the motion [Sec. 4, 
Rule 10, RRCTA];  
 
4. There is an appeal to the CTA from a CIR 
decision; and  
 
5. The appeal is not frivolous or dilatory. 
 
    iii. Injunction not available to restrain 
tax collection 
 
SECTION 218 OF THE NIRC 
 
GR: No court shall have the authority to grant 
an injunction to restrain the collection of any 
national internal revenue tax, fee or charge 
imposed by this Code. 
 
XPN: SEC. 11, R.A. NO. 1125 
 
The CTA has ample authority to dispense with 
the deposit of the amount claimed or the filing 
of the required bond, whenever the method 
employed by the BIR in the collection of tax 
jeopardizes the interest of the taxpayer for 
being patently in violation of law [Sps. 
Pacquiao v. CTA First Division, G.R. No. 
213394 (2016)]. 
 
c. Criminal cases 
 
i. Institution and Prosecution of Criminal 
Actions 
 
Institution of Criminal Action 
 
Institution by the filing of an information in the 
name of the People of the Philippines. (Sec. 2, 
Rule 9, RRCTA) 
 
The filing of criminal actions which involve 
violations of the NIRC and other laws enforced 
by the BIR must be approved by the CIR. (Sec. 
2, Rule 9, RRCTA) 
 
The filing of criminal actions which involve 
violations of the Tariff and Customs Code and 
other laws enforced by the Bureau of Customs 
(BOC) must be approved by the Commissioner 
of Customs (COC). (Sec. 2, Rule 9, RRCTA) 
 
Note: The institution of the criminal 
action shall interrupt the running of the 
period of prescription. (Sec. 2, Rule 9, 
RRCTA) 
 
Prosecution of Criminal Action 
 
All criminal actions shall be conducted and 
prosecuted under the direction and control of 
the public prosecutor. (Sec. 3, Rule 9, RRCTA) 
Prosecution of criminal actions involving 
violations of the NIRC or other laws enforced 
by the BIR, and violations of the Tariff and 
Customs Code or other laws enforced by the 
BOC, may be conducted by their respective 
duly deputized legal officers. 
 
 
ii. Institution of Civil Action in Criminal 
Action 
 
In cases within the jurisdiction of the Court, the 
criminal action and the corresponding civil 
action for the recovery of civil liability for taxes 
and penalties shall be deemed jointly instituted 
in the same proceeding. 
 
The filing of the criminal action shall 
necessarily carry with it the filing of the civil 
action. No right to reserve the filing of such civil 
action separately from the criminal action shall 
be allowed or recognized. (Sec. 11, Rule 9, 
RRCTA)
